# TIMELINE · Tretā Yuga

> **Period 4 of 9 on the cosmic clock.** One slice of the corpus-wide [TIMELINE hub](../TIMELINE.md); each entry links to the file where the event is detailed. Back to the [master index](../README.md).
> ← Prev: [Satya Yuga](02-satya-yuga.md) · [↑ TIMELINE hub](../TIMELINE.md) · Next: [Dvāpara Yuga](04-dvapara-yuga.md) →

**1166 events.**

*Dharma on three legs. Vāmana, Paraśurāma, and the Rāmāyaṇa.*

---

- **"I desire not the eight siddhis"** — giving away the last water, Rantideva renounces even liberation, asking only to enter into and bear the suffering of all embodied beings so they be freed from misery _(actors: Rantideva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rantideva.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9.21.12)
- **A Brāhmaṇa Is Adaṇḍya** — Rāma's councillors Bhṛgu, Aṅgiras, Kutsa, Vasiṣṭha and Kaśyapa rule that a brāhmaṇa cannot be corporally punished, forcing Rāma to let the plaintiff dog name the sentence _(actors: Rāma, Bhṛgu, Aṅgiras, Vasiṣṭha, Kaśyapa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramas-justice-the-dog-and-the-mendicant.md) — (Uttara Kāṇḍa, prakṣipta) [scholarly]
- **Agastya finds the corpse-eater at the empty lake** — wandering the deserted future-Daṇḍaka, Agastya sees a radiant heaven-dweller descend from a swan-chariot to devour a human corpse floating in a pristine lake _(actors: Agastya, King Śveta, apsarās)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shveta-corpse-king-and-agastya.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara Kāṇḍa 77)
- **Ahalyā's śāpa-mokṣa** — Gautama's curse on Ahalyā is capped with an end-condition and released by Rāma's presence, the classic curse-liberation _(actors: Ahalyā, Gautama, Indra, Rāma)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/tapas-boons-and-curses.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 1.48–49)
- **Aṅgada crowned king of Kiṣkindhā** — at Rāma's final departure Sugrīva follows him into retirement, first enthroning his nephew Aṅgada so the once-disinherited prince inherits his father Vālī's throne in full _(actors: Aṅgada, Sugrīva, Rāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/angada.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara-kāṇḍa tradition) [late text]
- **Aṅgada leads the fast unto death on the southern shore** — deadline broken and Sītā unfound, the despairing prince, recalling his father's killing and dreading Sugrīva's wrath, resolves with his party on prāyopaveśa rather than return in disgrace _(actors: Aṅgada, Hanumān, Jāmbavān)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/angada.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Kiṣkindhā-kāṇḍa 55–56)
- **Aṅgada leads the southern search-party** — Sugrīva entrusts the decisive southern quarter to Aṅgada, commanding Hanumān, Jāmbavān, Nīla and others to seek Sītā across the Vindhyas and the southern ocean within one month _(actors: Aṅgada, Hanumān, Jāmbavān, Sugrīva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/angada.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Kiṣkindhā-kāṇḍa 41)
- **Aṅgada made crown prince of Kiṣkindhā** — at Sugrīva's consecration by Lakṣmaṇa, Vālī's orphaned son Aṅgada is installed as yuvarāja (heir-apparent), fulfilling his father's dying wish and securing the succession without civil war _(actors: Aṅgada, Sugrīva, Lakṣmaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/angada.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Kiṣkindhā-kāṇḍa 25–26)
- **Aṅgada plants his immovable foot (Aṅgada-Rāyabāram)** — in vernacular retelling Aṅgada roots his foot in the assembly and challenges the whole court to lift it; every rākṣasa champion and Rāvaṇa himself fail, and he taunts the emperor to bow at Rāma's feet instead _(actors: Aṅgada, Rāvaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/angada.md) — (Kamba Rāmāyaṇam; Rāmcaritmānas, Laṅkā-kāṇḍa) [folk]
- **Aṅgada slays the rākṣasa Vajradaṃṣṭra** — in the successive sorties before Laṅkā's gates the crown prince kills the commander Vajradaṃṣṭra, fighting as the foremost vānara duellist after Hanumān _(actors: Aṅgada, Vajradaṃṣṭra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/angada.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Yuddha-kāṇḍa 51–58)
- **Aṅgada's defiant embassy to Rāvaṇa's court** — sent as Rāma's envoy demanding Sītā's release on pain of death and Vibhīṣaṇa's enthronement, Aṅgada is ordered seized, but leaps to the palace roof wrecking a turret and returns unharmed _(actors: Aṅgada, Rāvaṇa, Rāma, Vibhīṣaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/angada.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Yuddha-kāṇḍa 41)
- **Aśokasundarī curses Huṇḍa** — inviolate and enraged, she dooms the demon to die at the hands of the very Nahuṣa she is destined to marry, converting prophecy into fate _(actors: Aśokasundarī, Huṇḍa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ashokasundari-nahusha-hunda.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Bhūmi-khaṇḍa 108)
- **Bharata looses the Saṃvarta weapon** — unable to break the Gandharvas by ordinary arms, Bharata discharges the pralaya-scaled Saṃvarta astra and annihilates the host in a single stroke — three hundred thousand slain _(actors: Bharata, the Gandharvas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bharata-gandharva-conquest-takshashila.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara-kāṇḍa 101; the three-crore host under Śailūṣa is the Agni Purāṇa's, ch. 11)
- **Birth of Kuśanābha's hundred daughters** — King Kuśanābha of Mahodaya begets one hundred daughters of unmatched beauty on the apsarā Ghṛtācī _(actors: Kuśanābha, Ghṛtācī)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kushanabha-daughters-and-brahmadatta.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 32)
- **Birth of Mandodarī to Maya and Hemā** — the asura-architect Maya and the apsarā Hemā have a daughter, Mandodarī, left in Maya's care when Hemā is recalled to the celestial sphere _(actors: Mandodarī, Maya Dānava, Hemā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/mandodari.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara Kāṇḍa 12)
- **Birth of Śala, Dala and Vala** — Suśobhanā bears Parikṣit three sons under her father's curse, the eldest Śala succeeding to the Ikṣvāku throne _(actors: Suśobhanā, Parikṣit, Śala, Dala, Vala)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/manduka-frog-princess-sushobhana.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva CXCII)
- **Birth of Śūrpaṇakhā** — Śūrpaṇakhā is born to the sage Viśravas and the rākṣasī Kaikasī, youngest sibling of Rāvaṇa, Kumbhakarṇa and Vibhīṣaṇa _(actors: Śūrpaṇakhā, Viśravas, Kaikasī, Rāvaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/shurpanakha.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara Kāṇḍa 9–10)
- **Brahmā commands the creation of the vānara host** — the devas beget shape-shifting monkey-sons (and Jāmbavān springs from Brahmā's yawn) as an army for the coming Rāma-avatāra _(actors: Brahmā, the devas, Jāmbavān)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/vanaras-and-rikshas.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla-kāṇḍa 17)
- **Brahmā parts them and ends the feud** — the Grandfather descends, dissolves their brute-nature, restores the sages to human form, declares Viśvāmitra's ordeal had won Hariścandra heaven, and reconciles the rivals in an embrace _(actors: Brahmā, Vasiṣṭha, Viśvāmitra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/adi-baka-bird-war.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa IX; Devī-Bhāgavata VI.13)
- **Brahmā's oracle names Raji the pivot of the devāsura war** — Deadlocked devas and asuras both ask Brahmā who will win, and he answers: whichever side the mortal king Raji joins _(actors: Brahmā, Raji, devas, asuras)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/raji-and-his-sons.md) — (Vishnu Purana IV.9; Matsya Purana 24)
- **Brahmā's rebuke: tapas without dāna** — reaching Brahmaloka yet stricken by impossible hunger, Śveta is told by Brahmā that he gave no gifts, and is sentenced to eat his own endlessly-regenerating flesh daily _(actors: Śveta, Brahmā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shveta-corpse-king-and-agastya.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara Kāṇḍa 78)
- **Brahmadatta's marriage heals the hundred hunchbacked daughters** — Kuśanābha gives all hundred daughters to King Brahmadatta of Kāmpilya, and at the touch of his hand in the wedding rite each maiden's crookedness vanishes and her beauty returns _(actors: Brahmadatta, the hundred daughters, Kuśanābha, Somadā)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kushanabha-daughters-and-brahmadatta.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 33)
- **Breaking of Śiva's bow and wedding of Sītā** — escorted by Viśvāmitra to Mithilā, Rāma snaps the bow of Śiva and wins Sītā's hand in the quadruple wedding of Daśaratha's sons _(actors: Rāma, Sītā, Viśvāmitra, Janaka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/rama.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa)
- **Bṛhaspati's heterodox-delusion rites corrupt the Rājeyas** — The destitute Indra begs Bṛhaspati, who performs strengthening rites and promulgates a deluding anti-Vedic teaching that makes Raji's sons fall from dharma _(actors: Bṛhaspati, Indra, sons of Raji)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/raji-and-his-sons.md) — (Vishnu Purana IV.9; Matsya Purana 24; Bhagavata Purana 9.17)
- **Cirakārī deliberates too slowly to kill his mother** — ordered by the enraged Gautama to slay the adulterous Ahalyā, the habitually slow-acting son Cirakārī deliberates until his father repents, and slowness redeems them both _(actors: Gautama, Ahalyā, Cirakārī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shanti-parva-minor-upakhyanas.md) — (MBh, Śānti Parva 12.258)
- **Cūlī grants Somadā a mind-born son, Brahmadatta** — The gandharvī Somadā, daughter of Ūrmilā, serves the ascetic Cūlī and, being husbandless, asks as her boon a son by his tapas; the sage brings forth the mānasa son Brahmadatta, who rules Kāmpilya _(actors: Cūlī, Somadā, Brahmadatta)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kushanabha-daughters-and-brahmadatta.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 33)
- **Dala's poisoned arrow kills prince Senajit** — the defiant heir Dala aims a poisoned shaft at Vāmadeva, but by the sage's word it turns and slays his own ten-year-old son Senajit, and a second arrow freezes in his hand _(actors: Dala, Vāmadeva, Senajit)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/manduka-frog-princess-sushobhana.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva CXCIII)
- **Daśaratha rides into the sky** — by kingly merit and tapas Daśaratha mounts his war-chariot and ascends past the winds into the region of the nakṣatras to confront Saturn where he approaches Rohiṇī _(actors: Daśaratha, Śani)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dasharatha-shani-stotra-legend.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Nāgara-khaṇḍa)
- **Death of Dhruvasandhi and the Kosala usurpation** — the Solar-line king is killed by a lion while hunting, and Yudhājit of Ujjayinī slays Vīrasena of Kaliṅga to crown the younger prince Śatrujit at Ayodhyā _(actors: Dhruvasandhi, Yudhājit, Vīrasena, Śatrujit)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sudarshana-shashikala-navaratri.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa, III.14–15)
- **Devarāta receives Śiva's bow in trust** — The gods deposit Śiva's colossal bow (Pināka) as a nyāsa with king Devarāta of Videha, the heirloom on which the Rāmāyaṇa marriage-plot turns _(actors: Devarāta, the gods)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/videha-mithila-line-janaka.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla-kāṇḍa 66)
- **Durgā worship instituted at Vārāṇasī and Ayodhyā** — Subāhu wins the boon that the Goddess remain forever as Kāśī's guardian Durgā, and the restored King Sudarśana establishes her worship throughout Kosala — the text's charter of royal Devī cult _(actors: Subāhu, Sudarśana, the Devī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sudarshana-shashikala-navaratri.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa, III.25–26)
- **Fall of Rāvaṇa and the agni-parīkṣā** — Rāma slays Rāvaṇa at Laṅkā, then publicly repudiates Sītā, who is returned unscathed from the fire by Agni before Rāma accepts her _(actors: Rāma, Rāvaṇa, Sītā, Agni)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/rama.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Yuddha Kāṇḍa)
- **Founding of Puṣkalāvatī for Puṣkala** — in the adjacent Gandharva country Bharata founds Puṣkalāvatī (Charsadda) and installs his younger son Puṣkala, then dwells in the two cities five years before returning to Ayodhyā _(actors: Bharata, Puṣkala)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bharata-gandharva-conquest-takshashila.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara-kāṇḍa 101)
- **Founding of Takṣaśilā for Takṣa** — on the conquered Gāndhāra land Bharata raises the city of Takṣaśilā (Taxila) and consecrates his elder son Takṣa as its king _(actors: Bharata, Takṣa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bharata-gandharva-conquest-takshashila.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara-kāṇḍa 101)
- **Gaṇeśa grounds the Raṅganātha vimāna** — bearing the vimāna south after the Laṅkā war, Vibhīṣaṇa entrusts it at Tiruvaraṅgam to a cowherd boy (Gaṇeśa in disguise) who sets it on the earth, rooting Raṅganātha at Śrīraṅgam forever _(actors: Vibhīṣaṇa, Gaṇeśa, Raṅganātha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruchirappalli-rockfort-thayumanavar.md) — (Śrīraṅga-māhātmya)
- **Gaṇeśa installs Mahābala at Gokarṇa** — after Rāvaṇa wins Śiva's liṅga by penance and is tricked into setting it down, Gaṇanāyaka roots it at Gokarṇa as Mahābala, its power reaching to the seven nether worlds _(actors: Gaṇeśa, Rāvaṇa, Mahābala/Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kotirudra-minor-linga-legends.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṃhitā 8)
- **Gaṅgā is named at Devprayag** — the Bhāgīrathī of Bhagīratha's descent meets the Alaknandā, and from this confluence the united river is first called Gaṅgā _(actors: Gaṅgā, Bhagīratha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-prayag-alaknanda-confluences.md) — (Wikipedia, Panch Prayag/Devprayag; Kedāra-khaṇḍa tradition)
- **Haihaya–Kāśī wars and Pratardana's vengeance** — After the Vītahavyas/Haihayas crush Haryaśva, Sudeva and Divodāsa of Kāśī, Bharadvāja's putrakāmeṣṭi yields Pratardana, who exterminates the Vītahavyas; Vītahavya is made a brāhmaṇa by Bhṛgu _(actors: Divodāsa, Bharadvāja, Pratardana, Vītahavya, Bhṛgu)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kashi-line-divodasa.md) — (Mahābhārata, Anuśāsana Parva 30)
- **Hanumān mistakes Mandodarī for Sītā** — searching Rāvaṇa's sleeping palace, Hanumān momentarily takes the radiant queen Mandodarī for Sītā, then reasons the captive could never sleep amid luxury _(actors: Hanumān, Mandodarī, Sītā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/mandodari.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Sundara Kāṇḍa 9–11)
- **Hariścandra restored by the Ajā Ekādaśī** — the truth-king, enslaved at the cremation ground, keeps the Ajā fast on Gautama's counsel and regains son, wife, and kingdom _(actors: Hariścandra, Gautama)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ekadashi-mahatmyas-catalog.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Uttara-khaṇḍa 39–63) [late text]
- **Huṇḍa abducts Aśokasundarī** — the daitya lord of Mahodaya carries her off and tries to win her by conjured illusion, an āśrama and false ascetic she sees through _(actors: Huṇḍa, Aśokasundarī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ashokasundari-nahusha-hunda.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Bhūmi-khaṇḍa 106–108)
- **Huṇḍa kidnaps the infant Nahuṣa** — learning his destined slayer is born to King Āyu, Huṇḍa steals the newborn and orders him killed and cooked, but the cook's compassion spares the child _(actors: Huṇḍa, Nahuṣa, Āyu, the cook)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ashokasundari-nahusha-hunda.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Bhūmi-khaṇḍa 110–112)
- **Indra declares himself Raji's son to keep the throne** — Owing Raji the rank of Indra, Indra bows at his feet claiming 'you are my father,' and the gratified Raji leaves him functioning as king of heaven _(actors: Indra, Raji)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/raji-and-his-sons.md) — (Vishnu Purana IV.9; Matsya Purana 24)
- **Indra purified at Suchindram** — sin-stained from seducing Ahalyā and cursed by Gautama, Indra travels to the far-southern liṅga and is washed pure (*śuci*), giving the town its name *Śuci-Indram* _(actors: Indra, Gautama, Ahalyā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/suchindram-sthanumalayan.md) — (Sucīndra-sthala-māhātmya; cf. Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla 48–49)
- **Indra slays the apostate sons of Raji and regains heaven** — Once the deluded Rājeyas abandon the Veda and dharma, Indra kills all five hundred with the thunderbolt and recovers his station and sacrificial shares _(actors: Indra, sons of Raji, Bṛhaspati)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/raji-and-his-sons.md) — (Vishnu Purana IV.9; Bhagavata Purana 9.17; Matsya Purana 24)
- **Indra surrenders heaven to Raji for his lifetime** — In the Bhāgavata's variant, Indra, terrified of Prahlāda, hands the celestial kingdom back to Raji and clasps his feet, and Raji holds heaven until his death _(actors: Indra, Raji, Prahlāda)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/raji-and-his-sons.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 9.17)
- **Indra's endless night-watch** — because his cleansing is held to continue, the temple's late-night *ardhajāma* pūjā is understood as Indra's own worship, kept invisibly each night _(actors: Indra)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/suchindram-sthanumalayan.md) — (Sucīndra-sthala-māhātmya; temple custom)
- **Initiation into the inverted mantra "marā marā"** — too sin-laden for the Name itself, the penitent robber is set to repeating Rāma's name in reverse, which becomes Rāma on the tongue _(actors: the robber (Vālmīki-to-be), the Saptarṣi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/valmiki-ratnakara-conversion.md) — (Adhyātma Rāmāyaṇa, Ayodhyā-kāṇḍa 6)
- **Instigation of Khara and the battle of Janasthāna** — the mutilated Śūrpaṇakhā rouses Khara, whose fourteen champions and then full host of 14,000 with Dūṣaṇa and Triśiras are annihilated by Rāma single-handed _(actors: Śūrpaṇakhā, Khara, Dūṣaṇa, Triśiras, Rāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/shurpanakha.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Araṇya Kāṇḍa 19–30)
- **Jāmbavān awakens Hanumān's forgotten powers** — the aged bear-king, who once circumambulated Trivikrama, rouses Hanumān to leap the ocean to Laṅkā _(actors: Jāmbavān, Hanumān)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/vanaras-and-rikshas.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Kiṣkindhā-kāṇḍa 65–66)
- **Janaka gives Yājñavalkya the Videhas and himself** — Taught the Self as one's own light through waking, dream and deep sleep, and told 'you have attained the fearless, Janaka,' the king answers: 'I give you the Videhas, and myself with them, into servitude' _(actors: Janaka Vaideha, Yājñavalkya)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/videha-mithila-line-janaka.md) — (Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.1–4.4)
- **Janaka passes the agnihotra test to Yājñavalkya** — Janaka of Videha quizzes three brāhmaṇas on the agnihotra and drives off; Yājñavalkya pursues him, and the king wins the boon of unlimited questions — 'thenceforth Janaka was a brahman' _(actors: Janaka Vaideha, Yājñavalkya, Śvetaketu Āruṇeya, Somaśuṣma)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/videha-mithila-line-janaka.md) — (Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 11.6.2)
- **Kārtavīrya Arjuna's thousand-armed empire and fall** — The Haihaya-Yādava emperor of Māhiṣmatī, boon-armed by Dattātreya, seizes Jamadagni's wish-cow and is slain by Paraśurāma, triggering the twenty-one Kṣatriya exterminations _(actors: Kārtavīrya Arjuna, Dattātreya, Jamadagni, Paraśurāma)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/yadava-vamsha.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa IV.11; Bhāgavata IX.15-16, IX.23; Mahābhārata)
- **Kaśyapa and Aditi worship the icon** — reborn as Kaśyapa and Aditi, the couple keep the image and Viṣṇu is born to them as Upendra/Vāmana, the second of the three births _(actors: Kaśyapa, Aditi, Vāmana)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/guruvayur-krishna-kshetra.md) — (Guruvāyupura-māhātmya; Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.3)
- **Khaṭvāṅga wins the devas' war** — the unconquerable Ikṣvāku king Khaṭvāṅga, son of Viśvasaha and ancestor of Rāma, is summoned by the demigods to fight the asuras, secures their victory, and is offered any boon he wishes _(actors: Khaṭvāṅga, the devas, the asuras)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/khatvanga-one-muhurta.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9.9.41–42)
- **King Śveta of Vidarbha renounces for tapas** — Sudeva's son Śveta, after a thousand-year righteous reign, sets his brother Suratha on the throne and withdraws to austerities by the forest lake _(actors: Śveta, Sudeva, Suratha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shveta-corpse-king-and-agastya.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara Kāṇḍa 78)
- **Kuśa's four sons found their cities** — The Brahmā-born king Kuśa's sons Kuśāmba, Kuśanābha, Ādhūrtarajasa, and Vasu build Kauśāmbī, Mahodaya, Dharmāraṇya, and Girivraja respectively _(actors: Kuśa, Kuśāmba, Kuśanābha, Vasu)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kushanabha-daughters-and-brahmadatta.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 32)
- **Kuśanābha's putrakāmeṣṭi and the birth of Gādhi** — The sonless Kuśanābha performs a son-yielding sacrifice at which his father Kuśa promises a supremely righteous son, and Gādhi — Viśvāmitra's father — is born _(actors: Kuśanābha, Kuśa, Gādhi)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kushanabha-daughters-and-brahmadatta.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 34)
- **Kuśanābha's teaching of kṣamā** — Rather than avenging the god's assault, Kuśanābha praises his daughters' forbearance, declaring that on kṣamā the universe is established, and takes counsel on their marriage _(actors: Kuśanābha, the hundred daughters)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kushanabha-daughters-and-brahmadatta.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 33)
- **Liberation in a single muhūrta** — returning at once to earth, Khaṭvāṅga declares nothing dearer to him than Nārāyaṇa, casts off queens, kingdom and the guṇa-swayed senses, and merges in Vāsudeva/Brahman before the 48-minute hour is out _(actors: Khaṭvāṅga, Nārāyaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/khatvanga-one-muhurta.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9.9.44–49)
- **Makarākṣa slain by the Āgneyāstra** — Rāma fits the fire-weapon of Agni to his bow and pierces Makarākṣa through the chest, felling Khara's son as his father had fallen _(actors: Rāma, Makarākṣa, Agni)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/makaraksha-son-of-khara.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Yuddha Kāṇḍa 79)
- **Makarākṣa's vow of vengeance** — the son of Khara joyfully accepts the charge, vowing to avenge his father slain by Rāma at Janasthāna _(actors: Makarākṣa, Rāvaṇa, Khara)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/makaraksha-son-of-khara.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Yuddha Kāṇḍa 78)
- **Māṇḍakarṇi yields and weds all five** — unlike the wrathful Viśvāmitra, the sage succumbs to desire and takes the five apsaras as his wives rather than cursing them _(actors: Māṇḍakarṇi, five apsaras)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mandakarni-and-panchapsaras-lake.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Araṇya Kāṇḍa 11)
- **Māṇḍakarṇi's ten-thousand-year immersion** — the sage stands submerged in the lake living on air alone for ten thousand years, heaping up ascetic power that alarms the gods _(actors: Māṇḍakarṇi, the devas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mandakarni-and-panchapsaras-lake.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Araṇya Kāṇḍa 11)
- **Mandodarī bears Meghanāda (Indrajit)** — Laṅkā's crown prince is born to Rāvaṇa and Mandodarī, named for his thundercloud birth-cry _(actors: Mandodarī, Rāvaṇa, Meghanāda)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/mandodari.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara Kāṇḍa)
- **Mandodarī counsels Rāvaṇa to return Sītā** — with Vibhīṣaṇa and Mālyavān, Mandodarī urges restoring Sītā and peace with Rāma, declaring him no mere man; Rāvaṇa refuses _(actors: Mandodarī, Rāvaṇa, Vibhīṣaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/mandodari.md) — (Rāmāyaṇa tradition; Rāmcaritmānas, Sundara–Laṅkā Kāṇḍa)
- **Mandodarī remarries Vibhīṣaṇa (later tradition)** — at Rāma's counsel the widowed queen weds the newly crowned Vibhīṣaṇa, securing Laṅkā's throne; absent from the Vālmīki critical edition [late text] [disputed] _(actors: Mandodarī, Vibhīṣaṇa, Rāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/mandodari.md) — (later Purāṇic/regional Rāmāyaṇa tradition)
- **Mandodarī's lament over the slain Rāvaṇa** — widowed on the battlefield, Mandodarī names lust for Sītā as the cause of Laṅkā's ruin and recognises Rāma as the eternal Viṣṇu _(actors: Mandodarī, Rāvaṇa, Rāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/mandodari.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Yuddha Kāṇḍa 111)
- **Manoramā's flight to Bharadvāja's hermitage** — the widowed queen escapes Ayodhyā with the child-heir Sudarśana and the minister Vidalla, taking refuge under the sage's protection against Yudhājit's pursuit _(actors: Manoramā, Sudarśana, Vidalla, Bharadvāja, Yudhājit)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sudarshana-shashikala-navaratri.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa, III.15–17)
- **Nahuṣa fostered by Vasiṣṭha** — the rescued infant is laid in Vasiṣṭha's hermitage, where the sage and Arundhatī raise him and school him in the Vedas and arms _(actors: Nahuṣa, Vasiṣṭha, Arundhatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ashokasundari-nahusha-hunda.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Bhūmi-khaṇḍa 112–115)
- **Nahuṣa weds Aśokasundarī** — blessed by Śiva and Pārvatī, the Candravaṃśa king marries the Kalpavṛkṣa-born maiden, who in this Padma frame (not Virajā of the epics) becomes mother of Yayāti _(actors: Nahuṣa, Aśokasundarī, Yayāti)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ashokasundari-nahusha-hunda.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Bhūmi-khaṇḍa 119–120)
- **Nala bridges the sea to Laṅkā** — Viśvakarman's vānara son directs the building of the Setu in five days for Rāma's army _(actors: Nala, Rāma, the vānara host)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/vanaras-and-rikshas.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Yuddha-kāṇḍa 22)
- **Nārada boasts against Śiva's warning** — ignoring Śiva's counsel to tell no one, Nārada brags of his victory over lust to Brahmā and then to Viṣṇu, who resolves to humble him by māyā _(actors: Nārada, Śiva, Brahmā, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-moha-svayamvara.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Rudra-saṃhitā, Sṛṣṭi-khaṇḍa 2)
- **Nārada curses the mocking Rudra-gaṇas** — the two attendants of Śiva who laughed at his monkey face are cursed to be born as demons from a rākṣasa womb, with the mitigation that they will return to Śiva's side _(actors: Nārada, the two Rudra-gaṇas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-moha-svayamvara.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Rudra-saṃhitā, Sṛṣṭi-khaṇḍa 4)
- **Nārada recites the saṃkṣipta-Rāmāyaṇa to Vālmīki** — asked who is the perfectly virtuous man, Nārada answers "Rāma" and plants the seed of the epic _(actors: Vālmīki, Nārada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/valmiki-ratnakara-conversion.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 1)
- **Nārada's penance and the false conquest of Kāma** — Kāmadeva, sent by Indra, fails to break Nārada's Himalayan tapas at Śiva's hallowed spot, and the sage swells with pride _(actors: Nārada, Kāmadeva, Indra, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-moha-svayamvara.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Rudra-saṃhitā, Sṛṣṭi-khaṇḍa 2)
- **Nārada's three-fold curse on Viṣṇu** — the enraged sage curses Viṣṇu to a mortal birth, to grief in separation from his wife, and to monkey companions — the Śaiva seed of the Rāma avatāra, which Viṣṇu gladly accepts _(actors: Nārada, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-moha-svayamvara.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Rudra-saṃhitā, Sṛṣṭi-khaṇḍa 4)
- **Nikumbha's curse empties Vārāṇasī** — Dishonored by King Divodāsa, the gaṇa Nikumbha curses Vārāṇasī to stand deserted a thousand years, and Śiva and Pārvatī dwell in the emptied city while the king removes to the Gomatī _(actors: Nikumbha, Divodāsa, Suyaśā, Śiva)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kashi-line-divodasa.md) — (Harivaṃśa ~29; Vāyu Purāṇa)
- **Parashurama founds the Pura Mahadev yatra** — Parashurama carries Ganga water on foot from Garhmukteshwar to worship Shiva at Pura Mahadev, an alternative founding of the kanwar vow [folk] [regional] _(actors: Parashurama, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanwar-yatra-and-shravan-shiva-observances.md) — (Pura Mahadev/Garhmukteshwar temple tradition)
- **Paraśurāma reclaims Gomāntaka** — the axe-bearing avatar shoots an arrow into the sea, forces the waters back, and settles Brahmin gotras on the uncovered Konkan strip that becomes Goa _(actors: Paraśurāma, the ocean, Brahmin settlers)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/goa-shantadurga-mangeshi-kuladevatas.md) — (Sahyādri-khaṇḍa, Skanda Purāṇa)
- **Paraśurāma seeks out Dattātreya** — sick at heart after exterminating the kṣatriyas twenty-one times, the axe-wielder comes to his guru Dattātreya asking what world, bondage, and release truly are, and receives the Tripurā Rahasya _(actors: Paraśurāma, Dattātreya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/tripura-rahasya.md) — (Tripurā Rahasya, Māhātmya-khaṇḍa)
- **Paraśurāma's axe releases the Lauhitya** — bathing in the Brahmakuṇḍa frees Paraśurāma's axe and matricide-sin, and he cleaves the mountain wall to loose the river into the world _(actors: Paraśurāma)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lauhitya-brahmaputra-origin.md) — (Kālikā Purāṇa, 84–85)
- **Puruṣottama appears in the guise of an ascetic** — after Ratnagrīva's five-day fast at the Gaṅgā-ocean confluence the Lord comes disguised, promising darśana at noon and naming five who will reach the highest place _(actors: Puruṣottama/Viṣṇu, Ratnagrīva, Karamba the weaver)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ratnagriva-purushottama-kshetra.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Pātāla-khaṇḍa 21)
- **Raji destroys the asura host for the devas** — Fighting for the gods under the Indra-ship bargain, the Aila king Raji slays the asura armies and wins the war the devas could not _(actors: Raji, devas, asuras)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/raji-and-his-sons.md) — (Vishnu Purana IV.9; Bhagavata Purana 9.17; Matsya Purana 24)
- **Rājñā Devī carried from Laṅkā** — displeased with Rāvaṇa who first installed her, the goddess Rājñā (Ragnya) asks Hanumān to bear her seat north from Laṅkā to Tulmul, where she settles as Kṣīr Bhavānī _(actors: Rājñā/Ragnya Devī, Rāvaṇa, Hanumān)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kashmir-tirthas-nilamata-geography.md) — (Kṣīr Bhavānī sthala tradition)
- **Rāma accepts fourteen years' exile** — honouring Daśaratha's boons to Kaikeyī, Rāma renounces the throne without protest and departs for the forest with Sītā and Lakṣmaṇa _(actors: Rāma, Kaikeyī, Daśaratha, Bharata)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/rama.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Ayodhyā Kāṇḍa)
- **Rāma appoints Bharata over the Gandharva war** — Rāma assents to the conquest and dispatches Bharata with his two sons Takṣa and Puṣkala, the campaign framed from the outset as a city-founding for the next generation _(actors: Rāma, Bharata, Takṣa, Puṣkala)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bharata-gandharva-conquest-takshashila.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara-kāṇḍa 100)
- **Rāma breaks the bow; the quadruple wedding at Mithilā** — Rāma snaps Śiva's bow and the Videha and Ikṣvāku houses are knit fourfold: Rāma–Sītā, Lakṣmaṇa–Ūrmilā, Bharata–Māṇḍavī, Śatrughna–Śrutakīrti _(actors: Rāma, Sītā, Janaka, Daśaratha, Kuśadhvaja)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/videha-mithila-line-janaka.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla-kāṇḍa 67–73)
- **Rama carries water from Sultanganj to Baidyanath** — Eastern tradition holds Rama himself bore Ganga water from the uttaravahini Ganga at Sultanganj to the Vaidyanath Jyotirlinga at Deoghar, chartering the Shravani Mela route [folk] [regional] _(actors: Rama, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanwar-yatra-and-shravan-shiva-observances.md) — (Shravani Mela/Baidyanath tradition)
- **Rāma keeps the Vijayā Ekādaśī at the ocean** — counselled before the crossing to Laṅkā, Rāma keeps the Vijayā fast by whose merit the ocean is crossed and Rāvaṇa defeated _(actors: Rāma, Vakadālbhya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ekadashi-mahatmyas-catalog.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Uttara-khaṇḍa 39–63) [late text]
- **Rāma recognizes Satī and dispels her doubt** — Undeceived, Rāma salutes Satī by name as Dakṣa's daughter and asks why she wanders alone in Sītā's guise; her doubt collapses in shame _(actors: Rāma, Satī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sati-tests-rama.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Satī-khaṇḍa 24; Rāmcaritmānas, Bāl-kāṇḍa)
- **Rāma's akāl-bodhan founds the autumn Durgā Pūjā calendar** — Rāma awakens Durgā out of season in autumn and worships her from Ṣaṣṭhī to Daśamī before the Laṅkā war, the charter of Bengal's Śāradīyā festival days _(actors: Rāma, Durgā, Brahmā)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/durga-puja-bengal.md) — (Kṛttivāsī Rāmāyaṇ; Kālikā/Bṛhaddharma Purāṇa)
- **Rāma's Aśvamedha and the capture of the horse by Lava and Kuśa** — after the return to Ayodhyā the sacrificial horse wanders under Śatrughna's guard until Sītā's twin sons seize it at Vālmīki's āśrama, forcing recognition and reunion _(actors: Rāma, Śatrughna, Lava, Kuśa, Sītā)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/padma-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Pātāla-khaṇḍa)
- **Rāma's aśvamedha horse nears the Nīla mountain** — Śatrughna's army reaches the Puruṣottama region and the minister Sumati recounts the old tale of Ratnagrīva of Kāñcī _(actors: Śatrughna, Sumati, Śeṣa, Vātsyāyana)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ratnagriva-purushottama-kshetra.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Pātāla-khaṇḍa 17, 22–23)
- **Rāma's enlightenment and the charter of jīvanmukti** — Rāma attains repose in the Self, is roused from absorption, and chooses to rule and act while inwardly free, recasting the coming Rāmāyaṇa as a jīvanmukta's career _(actors: Rāma, Vasiṣṭha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yoga-vasishtha-tales-catalog.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha, Nirvāṇa Uttarārdha VI.2, final sargas)
- **Rāma's expiation at Devprayag** — Rāma performs penance at the Bhāgīrathī–Alaknandā confluence to cleanse the brahma-hatyā of killing Rāvaṇa; the Raghunāthjī temple marks the site [regional] _(actors: Rāma, Rāvaṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-prayag-alaknanda-confluences.md) — (Wikipedia, Devprayag; regional sthala-purāṇa)
- **Rāma's Navarātri before the war on Rāvaṇa** — after Sītā's abduction, Nārada prescribes the autumn Navarātra-vrata; Rāma performs it and the Devī grants victory, the recovery of Sītā, and long rule _(actors: Rāma, Nārada, the Devī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sudarshana-shashikala-navaratri.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa, III.28–30)
- **Rāma's offered eye (akāl-bodhan)** — the Śākta twin of the tale: worshipping Durgā before Laṅkā with 108 blue lotuses, Rāma finds one hidden and prepares to give his own lotus-eye until the Goddess stays his hand _(actors: Rāma, Durgā, Hanumān, Vibhīṣaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vishnu-lotus-eye-sudarshana.md) — (Kṛttivāsī Rāmāyaṇ)
- **Rāma's retort and the arrow-duel** — Rāma recalls slaying Khara, Dūṣaṇa, Triśiras and fourteen thousand rākṣasas alone at Janasthāna, then breaks Makarākṣa's chariot and horses in a shaft-storm _(actors: Rāma, Makarākṣa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/makaraksha-son-of-khara.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Yuddha Kāṇḍa 79)
- **Rāma-paṭṭābhiṣeka and Rāma-rājya** — Rāma is crowned at Ayodhyā and his reign becomes the proverbial ideal polity of dharma, without untimely death or want _(actors: Rāma, Sītā, Bharata, Hanumān)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/rama.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Yuddha/Uttara Kāṇḍa)
- **Rāmavaradāyinī — the boon to Rāma** — southbound in search of Sītā, Rāma is blessed by the goddess at Tuljapur, folding the kṣetra into Rāmāyaṇa geography _(actors: Rāma, the Devī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tuljapur-tulja-bhavani.md) — (sthala-tradition) [folk] [regional]
- **Rantideva keeps nothing for the morrow** — the Bhārata-line king, son of Saṅkṛti, gives away all that comes to him and lives in voluntary poverty, he and his household bearing want with perfect equanimity _(actors: Rantideva, Saṅkṛti's line)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rantideva.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9.21.3–4)
- **Ratnagrīva proclaims the pilgrimage to Puruṣottama** — minister Uttama announces the yātrā through Kāñcī and all four varṇas and occupational groups march east singing of Kṛṣṇa and Viṣṇu _(actors: Ratnagrīva, Uttama, the people of Kāñcī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ratnagriva-purushottama-kshetra.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Pātāla-khaṇḍa 19–20)
- **Ravana carries the first kanwar** — Ravana fetches Ganga water in a shoulder-pole kanwar and pours it on the Shiva-linga at Pura Mahadev (Baghpat), releasing Shiva from the poison's heat — the most-cited first-kanwariya charter [folk] _(actors: Ravana, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanwar-yatra-and-shravan-shiva-observances.md) — (Pura Mahadev local tradition)
- **Rāvaṇa dispatches Makarākṣa** — grieving his lost champions, Rāvaṇa sends Makarākṣa, son of the slain demon-lord Khara, out from Laṅkā to destroy the Rāghavas _(actors: Rāvaṇa, Makarākṣa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/makaraksha-son-of-khara.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Yuddha Kāṇḍa 78)
- **Rāvaṇa weds Mandodarī; Maya gifts the śakti** — meeting Maya in the forest, Rāvaṇa receives Mandodarī in marriage along with an unfailing śakti-javelin later hurled at Lakṣmaṇa _(actors: Mandodarī, Rāvaṇa, Maya Dānava)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/mandodari.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara Kāṇḍa 12)
- **Rāvaṇa's contempt-clause boon** — ten-thousand-year head-offering tapas wins invulnerability from all beings except the humans and monkeys he omits _(actors: Rāvaṇa, Brahmā)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/tapas-boons-and-curses.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara Kāṇḍa 7.10)
- **Rāvaṇa's funeral; Mandodarī's water-offering** — at Rāma's direction Vibhīṣaṇa performs full funeral rites and Mandodarī with the harem women makes the water-offering before Vibhīṣaṇa's coronation _(actors: Mandodarī, Vibhīṣaṇa, Rāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/mandodari.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Yuddha Kāṇḍa 111–112)
- **Ṛkṣarajas' pool-transformation begets Vālī and Sugrīva** — the first vānara, turned woman in an enchanted pool, bears Indra's son Vālī and Sūrya's son Sugrīva _(actors: Ṛkṣarajas, Indra, Sūrya)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/vanaras-and-rikshas.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara-kāṇḍa)
- **Śabarī awaits Rāma at Pampā Sarovar** — Directed by Kabandha, Rāma and Lakṣmaṇa reach the lake-side āśrama where the ascetic Śabarī, having long awaited Rāma's darśana, offers him fruit (famously pre-tasted berries) before attaining liberation _(actors: Rāma, Lakṣmaṇa, Śabarī, (Kabandha, sage Mataṅga))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kishkindha-hampi-anegundi.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Araṇya-kāṇḍa 70–74; folk amplification of the tasted berries)
- **Agastya directs Rāma to Pañcavaṭī** — Agastya gifts Rāma Viṣṇu's bow and divine weapons and names Pañcavaṭī on the Godāvarī as the exiles' dwelling-site _(actors: Rāma, Agastya, Lakṣmaṇa, Sītā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panchavati-nashik-godavari.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Araṇya-kāṇḍa 11–13)
- **Śala borrows Vāmadeva's Vāmī mares** — to run down an uncatchable deer King Śala borrows the wind-swift Vāmī mares of the ṛṣi Vāmadeva, on pledge to return them _(actors: Śala, Vāmadeva, the sūta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/manduka-frog-princess-sushobhana.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva CXCIII)
- **Śala slain by four Rākṣasas** — Śala breaks his word and keeps the mares as unfit for a brāhmaṇa, rebuffing Atreya and the sage, until Vāmadeva's tapas conjures four spear-armed Rākṣasas who kill him _(actors: Śala, Vāmadeva, Atreya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/manduka-frog-princess-sushobhana.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva CXCIII)
- **Sampāti sets Aṅgada's party on the path to Laṅkā** — the wingless vulture, roused by the lament for his brother Jaṭāyu, tells the fasting vānaras that Rāvaṇa holds Sītā in Laṅkā a hundred yojanas across the sea, giving the search its target _(actors: Sampāti, Aṅgada, Jaṭāyu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/angada.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Kiṣkindhā-kāṇḍa 56–59)
- **Saṃvarta the avadhūta unsettles Paraśurāma** — the naked, serene wanderer Saṃvarta, brother of Bṛhaspati, whose unaccountable bliss punctures the warrior's pride, points Paraśurāma toward the secret of the Goddess Tripurā _(actors: Paraśurāma, Saṃvarta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/tripura-rahasya.md) — (Tripurā Rahasya, Māhātmya-khaṇḍa)
- **Sarvārtha-siddha's Blow** — the brāhmaṇa mendicant Sarvārtha-siddha admits he struck the dog on the head while hungry on his alms-round because it blocked the road and would not move _(actors: Sarvārtha-siddha, the dog, Rāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramas-justice-the-dog-and-the-mendicant.md) — (Uttara Kāṇḍa, prakṣipta) [late text]
- **Śaśikalā's svayaṃvara and night wedding at Kāśī** — the Devī commands the Kāśī princess in a dream to wed Sudarśana; defying the assembled kings' threats, King Subāhu marries her to the exile privately in his palace _(actors: Śaśikalā, Sudarśana, Subāhu, Yudhājit)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sudarshana-shashikala-navaratri.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa, III.19–23)
- **Satī tests Rāma in Sītā's form** — Doubting that the weeping prince is Viṣṇu incarnate, Satī assumes Sītā's exact form and places herself in Rāma's path _(actors: Satī, Rāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sati-tests-rama.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Satī-khaṇḍa 24; Rāmcaritmānas, Bāl-kāṇḍa)
- **Satī's vision of multiplied Rāmas** — Withdrawing abashed, Satī sees countless Rāmas, Sītās and Lakṣmaṇas on every side, attended by all the gods — the one Lord reflected through creation _(actors: Satī, Rāma, Sītā, Lakṣmaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sati-tests-rama.md) — (Rāmcaritmānas, Bāl-kāṇḍa)
- **Śatrughna Slays Lavaṇāsura and Founds Mathurā** — Mathurā is said to be founded where Śatrughna, Rāma's brother, slays the asura Lavaṇa, son of Madhu, bridging the Rāmāyaṇa and Kṛṣṇa eras _(actors: Śatrughna, Lavaṇāsura)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mathura-vrindavan-braj.md) — (Ramayana, Uttara-kanda; Vishnu Purana)
- **Bali's Conquest of the Three Worlds** — Revived and empowered by Śukra and the Bhṛgus, the Asura king Bali storms Svarga and dispossesses Indra, becoming sovereign of all three worlds _(actors: Bali, Śukrācārya, Indra, Devas, Bhṛgu brahmins)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vamana-bali.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 8.15)
- **Bharadvāja receives Rāma on the exile journey** — On the vana-yātrā, Rāma, Sītā and Lakṣmaṇa cross the Yamunā and reach the sage Bharadvāja's āśrama at the Prayāga confluence; he honours them and directs them onward to Chitrakūṭa as the place of exile-dwelling _(actors: Bharadvāja, Rāma, Sītā, Lakṣmaṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/prayagraj-triveni-sangam.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Ayodhyā-kāṇḍa sargas ~50–56)
- **Birth and naming of Raghu** — Raghu is born to Dilipa and Sudakshina after Dilipa's service to the wish-cow Nandini, named 'the swift' for his future conquests _(actors: Raghu, Dilipa, Sudakshina, Nandini, Vasishtha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/raghu.md) — (Raghuvamsha 3.1-21)
- **Birth in the Bhargava line** — Paraśurāma, aṃśa of Viṣṇu, is born to the Bhṛgu sage Jamadagni and Reṇukā as the youngest of five sons _(actors: Jamadagni, Reṇukā, Paraśurāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/parashurama.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9.15; Mahābhārata 3.115)
- **Birth of Hanuman to Anjana and Vayu** — Hanuman is born to the apsara-turned-vanari Anjana and the vanara chief Kesari, conceived through the agency of Vayu, making him Vayuputra _(actors: Hanuman, Anjana, Kesari, Vayu)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/hanuman.md) — (Valmiki Ramayana, Kishkindha Kanda 66; Uttara Kanda 35-36)
- **Birth of Lakṣmaṇa and the pāyasa division** — From Daśaratha's putrakāmeṣṭi sacrifice a divine being yields sacred pāyasa; Sumitrā's two half-portions produce the twins Lakṣmaṇa and Śatrughna, Lakṣmaṇa attaching to Rāma _(actors: Daśaratha, Sumitrā, Lakṣmaṇa, Śatrughna)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/lakshmana.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla-kāṇḍa)
- **Birth of Māndhātā from His Father's Body** — The Ikṣvāku cakravartin Māndhātā is born from his father's body after the king drank consecrated fertility-water, and is suckled by Indra _(actors: Māndhātā, Yuvanāśva, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/minor-upakhyanas-catalog.md) — (Mahabharata Vana & Drona Parvas; Bhagavata 9; Vishnu Purana)
- **Birth of Rāvaṇa and his siblings** — Approaching Viśravas at an inauspicious twilight, Kaikasī bears the ten-headed Rāvaṇa (marked by ill omens), Kumbhakarṇa, the righteous Vibhīṣaṇa, and Śūrpaṇakhā _(actors: Viśravas, Kaikasī, Rāvaṇa, Kumbhakarṇa, Vibhīṣaṇa, Śūrpaṇakhā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/ravana.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara-kāṇḍa)
- **Birth of Sītā from the furrow** — Ploughing a sacrificial field, King Janaka turns up an infant girl in the furrow and adopts her as Sītā, an ayonijā born of the Earth-goddess _(actors: Janaka, Sītā, Bhūmi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/sita.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa)
- **Birth of Subāhu to Tāṭakā and Sunda** — Subāhu is born of the yakṣiṇī Tāṭakā by the yakṣa Sunda, brother of Mārīca, in the line cursed by Agastya _(actors: Subāhu, Tāṭakā, Sunda, Mārīca, Agastya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/subahu.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 25)
- **Birth of Tāṭakā from Suketu** — The childless yakṣa Suketu wins from Brahmā a daughter, Tāṭakā, endowed with the strength of a thousand elephants _(actors: Suketu, Brahmā, Tāṭakā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/tataka.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 25)
- **Birth of the Shloka Meter (Valmiki's Curse)** — Grieving a hunter's killing of a mating krauncha bird, Valmiki utters a curse that spontaneously takes metrical form, birthing the shloka (shoka to shloka) — the meter of Sanskrit epic _(actors: Valmiki, the hunter, krauncha birds)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/README.md) — (Valmiki Ramayana, Bala Kanda 2)
- **Birth of Vālī and Sugrīva from Ṛkṣaraja** — The vānara lord Ṛkṣaraja is transformed into a woman; Indra's seed on his hair begets Vālī and Sūrya's on his nape begets Sugrīva _(actors: Ṛkṣaraja, Indra, Sūrya, Vālī, Sugrīva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/sugriva-and-vali.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara-kāṇḍa)
- **Birth of Ṛśyaśṛṅga from a doe** — Vibhāṇḍaka's seed is swallowed by a doe, who bears a single-horned boy raised in total isolation, ignorant of women, his chastity amassing great tapas _(actors: Vibhāṇḍaka, the doe, Ṛśyaśṛṅga)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rishyashringa.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva ~110)
- **Choice of Citrakūṭa on Bharadvāja's counsel** — After crossing the Gaṅgā with Guha and reaching Bharadvāja's Prayāga āśrama, Rāma is directed to settle at Citrakūṭa as the ideal tapovana _(actors: Rāma, Sītā, Lakṣmaṇa, Guha the Niṣāda, sage Bharadvāja)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/chitrakoot-kamadgiri.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Ayodhyā-kāṇḍa 54)
- **Ṛcīka's swapped oblations and the birth of Jamadagni** — Two caru-porridges meant for brahmin and warrior sons are exchanged between Satyavatī and her mother, deferring the kṣatriya temper a generation so Jamadagni is born serene but Paraśurāma becomes the axe-bearer _(actors: Ṛcīka, Satyavatī, Gādhi's wife, Jamadagni (unborn))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/jamadagni-parashurama.md) — (Mahābhārata 3.115; Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9.15)
- **Counsel at the putrakāmeṣṭi — relaying the Sanatkumāra prophecy** — Sumantra privately advises childless Daśaratha, recalling Sanatkumāra's forecast about Ṛṣyaśṛṅga, prompting the son-seeking sacrifice sealed by Vasiṣṭha _(actors: Sumantra, Daśaratha, Vasiṣṭha, Ṛṣyaśṛṅga (referenced), Sanatkumāra (prophecy))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/sumantra.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla-kāṇḍa ~8–11)
- **Daśaratha resolves to crown Rāma yuvarāja** — The aged Daśaratha resolves to install Rāma as crown prince while he lives; the assembly acclaims the choice and the rite is rushed under Puṣya nakṣatra _(actors: Daśaratha, Rāma, Vasiṣṭha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/2-ayodhya-kanda.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Ayodhyā Kāṇḍa 1–4)
- **Daśaratha's righteous reign as last pre-Rāma Ikṣvāku king** — Daśaratha, son of Aja and grandson of Raghu, reigns at Ayodhyā as the last Sūryavaṃśa king before Rāma, prosperous but childless, served by Vasiṣṭha and his ministers _(actors: Daśaratha, Aja, Raghu, Vasiṣṭha, Kausalyā, Kaikeyī, Sumitrā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/dasharatha.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa Bāla 5–8; Bhāgavata Purāṇa IX.10; Kālidāsa Raghuvaṃśa 8–9)
- **Descent of the Gaṅgā at Gaṅgā-dvāra** — The Gaṅgā debouches from the Himalaya onto the plain at Haridwar, the gateway by which the goddess first enters the world of men, linked to Bhagīratha's bringing-down of the river _(actors: Gaṅgā, Bhagīratha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/haridwar-rishikesh-ganga-dwara.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva)
- **Descent of the Sarayū from Brahmā's Mānasa lake** — The Sarayū flows forth from Brahmā's Mānasa(rovara) lake, whence its name (sarat, 'flows out'), and runs to meet the Gaṅgā _(actors: Brahmā, Sarayū (river); pointed out by Viśvāmitra to Rāma and Lakṣmaṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sarayu-ayodhya-river.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla-kāṇḍa 23–24; (variant) Skanda Purāṇa Ayodhyā-māhātmya — Viṣṇu's foot-thumb)
- **Devas petition Brahmā for the Nāṭyaveda** — At the Tretā-yuga's dawn the gods ask Brahmā for an accessible 'fifth Veda' diversion open to all four varṇas _(actors: Devas, Brahmā)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/natya-shastra-and-the-classical-arts.md) — (Nāṭya Śāstra ch. 1)
- **Dilīpa's childlessness and the journey to Vasiṣṭha** — The ideal Sūryavaṃśa king Dilīpa, childless despite a flawless reign, drives with queen Sudakṣiṇā to Vasiṣṭha's hermitage to seek the cause and cure _(actors: Dilīpa, Sudakṣiṇā, Vasiṣṭha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dilipa-nandini.md) — (Raghuvaṃśa 1.12–58 (Kālidāsa))
- **Ganga springs from Vishnu's foot (Vamana)** — When Vamana's raised step pierces the cosmic shell, the Causal Ocean pours in and washes his foot, becoming the Ganga, hence Vishnupadi _(actors: Ganga, Vamana/Vishnu)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganga.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 5.17)
- **Gādhi's place in the Kuśika line** — Gādhi, Lunar-dynasty king of Kānyakubja in the Kuśika branch, stands as the genealogical hinge-node father of Viśvāmitra and Satyavatī _(actors: Gādhi, Kuśa, Kuśanābha, ancestors of the Kuśika line)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/gadhi-and-the-caru-swap.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa IV.7; Bhāgavata Purāṇa IX.15; Rāmāyaṇa Bāla Kāṇḍa 32–34; Mahābhārata Ādi & Vana Parva)
- **Guha welcomes Rama at Shringaverapura** — The Nishada chief Guha rushes out, embraces his friend Rama, and offers his whole kingdom; Rama accepts only water and fodder _(actors: Guha, Rama, Sita, Lakshmana, Nishadas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/guha.md) — (Valmiki Ramayana Ayodhya-kanda 50)
- **Hanumān meets Rāma and Lakṣmaṇa** — Sent by the anxious Sugrīva, Hanumān approaches the brothers disguised as a mendicant, is reassured of their good faith, and carries them on his shoulders to Sugrīva _(actors: Hanumān, Rāma, Lakṣmaṇa, Sugrīva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/4-kishkindha-kanda.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Kiṣkindhā Kāṇḍa 3–4)
- **Hanumān's leap across the ocean** — From Mount Mahendra Hanumān launches a hundred-yojana leap across the southern ocean toward Laṅkā _(actors: Hanumān)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/5-sundara-kanda.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Sundara Kāṇḍa 1)
- **Hanumān's leap and search of Laṅkā** — Hanumān crosses the ocean, searches Rāvaṇa's city by night, and comes upon the Aśoka-vāṭikā _(actors: Hanumān, Rāvaṇa (city))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lanka-ramayana-trail-ashoka-vatika.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Sundara Kāṇḍa 14–15)
- **Hariścandra's vow to Varuṇa and Rohita's birth** — Childless Hariścandra vows to sacrifice his firstborn to Varuṇa; a son Rohita is born, but the king endlessly postpones the offering _(actors: Hariścandra, Varuṇa, Rohita, Nārada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sunahshepa.md) — (Aitareya Brāhmaṇa 7.13-14)
- **Harishchandra's vow to Varuna for a son** — Childless King Harishchandra prays to Varuna and vows to sacrifice his promised son; Varuna grants the boon and Rohita is born _(actors: Harishchandra, Varuna, Rohita, Narada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sunahshepa.md) — (Aitareya Brahmana 7.13-14)
- **Satyavatī becomes the river Kauśikī** — Viśvāmitra tells how his elder sister Satyavatī, wife of Ṛcīka, followed her husband to heaven in her very body and became the Himalayan river Kauśikī (Kosi), by whose banks he dwells _(actors: Satyavatī, Ṛcīka, Viśvāmitra)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kushanabha-daughters-and-brahmadatta.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 34)
- **Shravan Kumar bears his parents in a kavad** — The devoted son carries his blind parents in slung baskets on a shoulder-yoke and is accidentally shot by Dasharatha; pilgrimage lore makes his yoke the prototype of the kanwar _(actors: Shravan Kumar, Dasharatha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanwar-yatra-and-shravan-shiva-observances.md) — (Valmiki Ramayana, Ayodhya Kanda 57–58)
- **Śibi and the dove — the flesh-offering** — Indra (hawk) and Agni (dove) test King Śibi, who protects the refugee dove by offering his own flesh equal to its weight, finally his whole body _(actors: Śibi, Indra, Agni)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shibi.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva ~130-131)
- **Indumatī's svayaṃvara at Vidarbha** — At her bride-choice, Indumatī passes the gallery of suitor-kings and garlands Aja, choosing him before all the assembled royalty _(actors: Aja, Indumatī, Sunandā, the suitor-kings of Magadha/Aṅga/Avantī/etc.)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/aja-indumati.md) — (Kālidāsa, Raghuvaṃśa canto 6)
- **Jabali counsels Rama (materialist argument)** — The brahmin Jabali urges Rama with proto-Carvaka materialist arguments (no afterlife, ritual for the dead is futile); Rama rebukes him _(actors: Jabali, Rama)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/nastika-darshanas-and-the-astika-nastika-frame.md) — (Ramayana, Ayodhya Kanda 108-109)
- **Janaka's debate-assembly convened** — King Janaka of Videha assembles the learned Brahmins at a sacrifice, offering a thousand gold-horned cattle to the greatest knower of Brahman; Yājñavalkya claims the prize, provoking eight challengers _(actors: King Janaka of Videha, Yājñavalkya, the assembled Brahmins)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/gargi-and-maitreyi.md) — (Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.1)
- **Kabandha's double curse and disfigurement** — The celestial being (Viśvāvasu/Danu) is struck by Indra's vajra into a headless trunk and cursed by sage Sthūlaśiras to remain a monster until Rāma severs his arms and burns him _(actors: Viśvāvasu/Kabandha, Indra, Sthūlaśiras)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/kabandha.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Araṇya-kāṇḍa 71)
- **Kaikasī's washed-away earthen liṅga** — Rāvaṇa's mother Kaikasī loses her daily sand liṅga to the tide and Rāvaṇa vows to fetch Śiva's true Ātma-liṅga _(actors: Kaikasī, Rāvaṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/gokarna-mahabaleshwar-atma-linga.md) — (Gokarṇa-māhātmya; Sahyādri tradition)
- **Kaikeyī's marriage-condition** — Aśvapati gives Kaikeyī to Daśaratha on the condition that her son inherit Ayodhyā's throne _(actors: Aśvapati, Daśaratha, Kaikeyī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/kaikeyi.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Ayodhyā 107/99)
- **Kauśika's hospitality at Vasiṣṭha's hermitage** — King Kauśika, resting with his army, is feasted entirely by Vasiṣṭha's wish-cow Śabalā/Kāmadhenu, igniting his covetousness for it _(actors: Viśvāmitra (Kauśika), Vasiṣṭha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vishwamitra-vasishtha.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa Bāla Kāṇḍa 51-53)
- **Kāmadhenu conflict — the spark** — King Kauśika tries to seize Vasiṣṭha's wish-cow Śabalā/Nandinī by force after the sage refuses to sell her _(actors: Viśvāmitra (Kauśika), Vasiṣṭha, Śabalā/Nandinī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vishwamitra-vasishtha.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa Bāla Kāṇḍa 51-55; Mahābhārata)
- **Lakṣmaṇa's request for liberating knowledge** — After Rāma's coronation at Ayodhyā, Lakṣmaṇa asks his brother to teach him the bhakti and jñāna by which one crosses saṃsāra, supplying the Gītā's seeker-frame _(actors: Rāma, Lakṣmaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rama-gita.md) — (Adhyātma Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara Kāṇḍa (book 7) sarga 5)
- **Mantharā sees Ayodhyā festive** — Mantharā climbs the palace terrace and learns Rāma is to be consecrated yuvarāja the next dawn _(actors: Mantharā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/manthara.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Ayodhyā Kāṇḍa 7)
- **Mataṅga's disciples promise Śabarī that Rāma will come** — The aged tribal ascetic Śabarī, having humbly served Mataṅga's hermitage, is blessed by the departing sages and told to wait at Pampā for Rāma _(actors: Śabarī, sage Mataṅga and his disciples)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/shabari.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa Araṇya-kāṇḍa 74)
- **Mārīca and Subāhu defile Viśvāmitra's sacrifice** — Mārīca and his brother Subāhu rain flesh and blood on Viśvāmitra's forest yajña, which the boy-princes Rāma and Lakṣmaṇa are sent to guard _(actors: Mārīca, Subāhu, Viśvāmitra, Rāma, Lakṣmaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/maricha.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla-kāṇḍa 19-26)
- **Nahuṣa installed as Indra** — After Indra flees the sin of slaying Vṛtra, the gods elect the virtuous mortal emperor Nahuṣa to the vacant throne of heaven, augmenting his tapas _(actors: Nahuṣa, Indra, the devas, ṛṣis)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nahusha.md) — (Mahābhārata Udyoga Parva 11-12; Bhāgavata 6.13)
- **Śāntā fostered from Ayodhyā to Aṅga** — Daśaratha of Ayodhyā gives his daughter Śāntā (born to Kausalyā), elder sister of Rāma, as a foster/adopted daughter to his childless friend King Lomapāda of Aṅga, where she is raised as the Aṅga princess _(actors: Śāntā, Daśaratha, Kausalyā, Lomapāda (Romapāda))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rishyashringa.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana-parva ~113; Rāmāyaṇa Bāla-kāṇḍa ~9–11; Purāṇic genealogies)
- **Paraśurāma Fills Five Lakes with Kṣatriya Blood** — Between Tretā and Dvāpara, Paraśurāma in his twenty-one exterminations of the kṣatriyas fills five lakes at Samanta-pañcaka with their blood and offers tarpaṇa, naming the field _(actors: Paraśurāma)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kurukshetra-dharmakshetra.md) — (Mahabharata, Adi-parva 2)
- **Paraśurāma receives the Paraśu from Śiva** — After his austerities, Paraśurāma is gifted Śiva's battle-axe (paraśu), the name-giving weapon of the sixth Daśāvatāra ('Rāma-with-the-axe'), with which he later annihilated the kṣatriya warrior-class twenty-one times over _(actors: Śiva, Paraśurāma)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/divyastras-and-divine-weapons.md) — (Mahābhārata; Purāṇic Paraśurāma cycle)
- **Paraśurāma retires to Mahendragiri** — After exterminating the kṣatriyas twenty-one times and ceding the conquered earth (to Kaśyapa / to Rāma Dāśarathi), the immortal sixth avatāra Paraśurāma withdraws from worldly action and retires to Mahendragiri in the Eastern Ghāts to perform tapas until the end of the age, making the peak a living Paraśurāma-tīrtha _(actors: Paraśurāma, Kaśyapa, Rāma Dāśarathi)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/meru-kailash-and-cosmic-mountains.md) — (Mahābhārata (Vana-parva, Śānti-parva); Rāmāyaṇa (Bāla-kāṇḍa))
- **Puṣpaka halted over Kailāsa** — Rāvaṇa's aerial car Puṣpaka is mysteriously halted as he crosses Mount Kailāsa returning from conquest _(actors: Rāvaṇa, Puṣpaka (vimāna))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ravana-kailasa-ravananugraha.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttarakāṇḍa 16)
- **Purūravas born of Budha and Ilā** — Purūravas is born to Budha (son of the Moon) and Ilā, Manu's gender-shifting child, grafting Manu's line onto the Moon's at the hinge of the Lunar dynasty _(actors: Purūravas, Budha, Ilā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pururavas-urvashi.md) — (Bhagavata Purana IX.1, IX.14; Vishnu Purana IV.1, IV.6)
- **Putrakāmeṣṭi and the divine pāyasa** — Daśaratha's son-seeking sacrifice via Ṛṣyaśṛṅga yields divine pāyasa from the fire to be shared among the queens _(actors: Daśaratha, Ṛṣyaśṛṅga, Yajña-puruṣa, Kausalyā, Kaikeyī, Sumitrā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/sumitra.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla-kāṇḍa 15-16)
- **Putrakāmeṣṭi sacrifice and the divine pāyasa** — Daśaratha performs the son-seeking sacrifice; a being from the fire brings golden pāyasa to beget heirs in whom Viṣṇu descends _(actors: Daśaratha, Ṛṣyaśṛṅga, Agni/divine messenger, the three queens)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/kausalya.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 14–16)
- **Reṇukā's chastity and the magic pot** — Reṇukā, chaste wife of Jamadagni, carries water in an unbaked sand pot held together by her faith alone _(actors: Reṇukā, Jamadagni)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/renuka-yellamma.md) — (Mahābhārata 3.116; Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa; folk Māhātmyas)
- **Reṇukā's unbaked-pot chastity feat** — Reṇukā, by the power of her perfect chastity, daily moulds a fresh unbaked clay/sand pot at the riverbank and carries water in it back to Jamadagni's hermitage, sometimes balancing it on a live coiled cobra _(actors: Reṇukā, Jamadagni)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Mahābhārata 3 (Vana-parvan); Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa; South Indian folk Māhātmyas)
- **Rāma despondent before the final duel** — On the battlefield before Laṅkā, a war-weary and anxious Rāma faces Rāvaṇa advancing for the climactic duel _(actors: Rāma, Rāvaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/aditya-hridayam.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Yuddha-kāṇḍa 105.1–4)
- **Rāma dissolves the Setu with his bow-tip at Dhanuṣkoḍi** — After the war, at Vibhīṣaṇa's request so the causeway could not be reused for invasion, Rāma touches the tip of his bow (dhanuṣ-koḍi) to the Setu to dissolve it, naming the southernmost headland of Pamban Island _(actors: Rāma, Vibhīṣaṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/rameswaram-setu-bandha.md) — (Rāmanātha sthala-purāṇa; Sethu Purāṇa (Tamil tradition))
- **Rāma exhausted on the battlefield** — After a punishing day of war, Rāma stands worn out and anxious before the undefeated Rāvaṇa for the final duel _(actors: Rāma, Rāvaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/aditya-hridayam.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Yuddha Kāṇḍa 105.1)
- **Rāma reaches Śṛṅgaverapura; Guha welcomes him** — On the first stage of exile Rāma arrives at the Niṣāda town on the Gaṅgā and Guha, chief of the Niṣādas and Rāma's friend, comes out with gifts and offers his kingdom _(actors: Rāma, Sītā, Lakṣmaṇa, Guha (Niṣāda king))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sringaverapura-guha-ganga-crossing.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Ayodhyā-kāṇḍa 50; Rāmcaritmānas, Ayodhyā-kāṇḍa)
- **Rāma singles out the Māṇḍūkya for Hanumān** — In the Muktikā dialogue Rāma instructs Hanumān on a graded path of Upaniṣadic study, declaring 'the Māṇḍūkya alone is enough for the liberation of the seeker.' _(actors: Rāma, Hanumān)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/mandukya-upanishad.md) — (Muktikā Upaniṣad 1.26–29)
- **Rāma threatens the ocean and Setu is built** — When the sea-lord refuses passage to Laṅkā, Rāma readies the brahmāstra; Varuṇa appears, submits, and advises building Rāma Setu with Nala's help _(actors: Rāma, Varuṇa/Samudra, Nala)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/varuna.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa Yuddha Kāṇḍa)
- **Rāvaṇa's digvijaya reaches Ayodhyā** — In his world-conquest Rāvaṇa marches against the Solar kings and comes to Anaraṇya, reigning Ikṣvāku sovereign of Ayodhyā _(actors: Rāvaṇa, Anaraṇya)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/anaranya-ravana-curse.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara Kāṇḍa 19 (within 18–23))
- **Satyavrata commits the three sins** — As a wild prince Satyavrata angers his father, abducts a bride, and kills and eats Vasishtha's sacred cow, for which Vasishtha curses and names him Trishanku _(actors: Satyavrata, Trayyaruna, Vasishtha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/trishanku.md) — (Harivamsha; Puranic)
- **Sītā born to Mandodarī and exposed (Jain/Śākta variant)** — in Guṇabhadra's Uttarapurāṇa, Saṅghadāsa's Vasudevahiṇḍī and the Adbhuta Rāmāyaṇa, Sītā is Mandodarī's daughter, abandoned over a doom-prophecy and found by Janaka [regional] [late text] _(actors: Mandodarī, Rāvaṇa, Sītā, Janaka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/mandodari.md) — (Uttarapurāṇa; Vasudevahiṇḍī; Adbhuta Rāmāyaṇa)
- **Sītā found in the furrow** — Ploughing the sacrificial ground, Sīradhvaja Janaka uncovers an infant girl in the furrow (sītā) and raises the ayonijā Earth-daughter as his own _(actors: Sīradhvaja Janaka, Sītā, Bhūmi)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/videha-mithila-line-janaka.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla-kāṇḍa 66)
- **Śiva bows to the grieving Rāma in the Daṇḍaka forest** — Roaming with Satī, Śiva salutes the Sītā-bereft Rāma from afar without revealing himself, planting Satī's doubt about Rāma's divinity _(actors: Śiva, Satī, Rāma, Lakṣmaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sati-tests-rama.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Rudra-saṃhitā, Satī-khaṇḍa 24; Rāmcaritmānas, Bāl-kāṇḍa)
- **Śiva mentally renounces Satī as wife** — Because Satī wore the form of Sītā, the consort of his chosen Lord, Śiva takes an inner vow of estrangement, acclaimed by a celestial voice that Satī overhears in grief _(actors: Śiva, Satī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sati-tests-rama.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Satī-khaṇḍa 25; Rāmcaritmānas, Bāl-kāṇḍa)
- **Slander of Sītā in Ayodhyā** — A washerman's grumble about taking back a wife who lived in another man's house becomes public censure (loka-apavāda) that Rāma the king cannot ignore _(actors: Rāma's spies, citizens, the washerman)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/uttara-ramayana-lava-kusha.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa Uttara Kāṇḍa 43-45)
- **Slaying of Virādha** — The rākṣasa Virādha seizes Sītā; the brothers bury him alive, and dying he reverts to the gandharva Tumburu, released by Rāma's touch _(actors: Rāma, Lakṣmaṇa, Virādha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/3-aranya-kanda.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Araṇya Kāṇḍa 2–4)
- **Sītā's abduction and Rāma's grief (frame)** — After Rāvaṇa carries off Sītā, Rāma wanders the Daṇḍaka forest in despair, the crisis that occasions the Gītā _(actors: Rāma, Sītā, Rāvaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shiva-gita.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Śiva-Gītā 1; cf. Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa Araṇya Kāṇḍa)
- **Sītā's earth-birth in the furrow of Mithilā** — King Janaka, ploughing the sacrificial field, lifts an infant girl (Sītā) from the furrow and takes her as his daughter; she is ayonijā, born of Earth _(actors: Janaka (Sīradhvaja), Sītā (Jānakī, Maithilī), Bhūmi (Earth))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/janakpur-mithila-sita-janmabhumi.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla-kāṇḍa 66; Adhyātma Rāmāyaṇa (Lakṣmī variant); Adbhuta Rāmāyaṇa & Jaina tellings (Rāvaṇa's-daughter variant))
- **Sudarśana receives the Kāmarāja bīja** — the exiled boy, mishearing the word "klība," unknowingly repeats the seed-syllable "klīṁ," and the pleased Goddess appears, granting him divine bow, inexhaustible arrows, and armour _(actors: Sudarśana, the Devī, Vidalla)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sudarshana-shashikala-navaratri.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa, III.17–19)
- **Sudeva of Vaidiśa dies sonless and becomes a preta** — a pious vaiśya merchant, lacking any heir to perform the sixteen monthly śrāddhas, is fixed in ghosthood despite a life of merit _(actors: Sudeva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/babhruvahana-and-the-preta.md) — (Garuḍa Purāṇa, Preta-khaṇḍa 4; Sāroddhāra 7.34–41)
- **Śūrpaṇakhā goads Rāvaṇa in Laṅkā** — she taunts Rāvaṇa with the fall of Janasthāna and dwells on Sītā's beauty, steering him toward the abduction that dooms Laṅkā _(actors: Śūrpaṇakhā, Rāvaṇa, Sītā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/shurpanakha.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Araṇya Kāṇḍa 32–34)
- **Suśobhanā vanishes into the tank** — the thirsty queen, led to water when the vow is forgotten, plunges in and disappears, leaving only a single frog behind _(actors: Parikṣit, Suśobhanā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/manduka-frog-princess-sushobhana.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva CXCII)
- **Śveta freed by Agastya's acceptance of the ornament** — Śveta presses a celestial ornament on Agastya as the price of deliverance, and the instant the sage accepts it his self-consuming body melts away and he ascends to Svarga _(actors: Śveta, Agastya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shveta-corpse-king-and-agastya.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara Kāṇḍa 78)
- **Years of war at Lake Mānasa** — nesting on trees by Mānasarovara, the two ṛṣi-birds fight for many years with beaks, wings, and nails, bloodied red as Kiṃśuka trees _(actors: Āḍi/Vasiṣṭha, Baka/Viśvāmitra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/adi-baka-bird-war.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata VI.13)
- **The 108 lotuses become the Sandhi Pūjā offering** — Rāma's offering of 108 blue lotuses (and his readiness to give his own eye) is the prototype of the 108 lamps and lotuses offered at Sandhi Pūjā _(actors: Rāma, Hanumān, Durgā)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/durga-puja-bengal.md) — (Kṛttivāsī Rāmāyaṇ)
- **The Āḍi and Baka grow titanic** — Vasiṣṭha's mainā swells to two thousand yojanas and Viśvāmitra's heron to three thousand and ninety, two colossal birds joined in combat _(actors: Vasiṣṭha as Āḍi, Viśvāmitra as Baka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/adi-baka-bird-war.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa IX)
- **The anthill rebirth — the robber emerges as Vālmīki** — after ages of motionless japa an anthill (valmīka) covers him; the returning sages call him out and name him Vālmīki _(actors: Vālmīki, the Saptarṣi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/valmiki-ratnakara-conversion.md) — (Adhyātma Rāmāyaṇa 2.6; variant: Skanda Purāṇa, Vaiśākhamāsa-māhātmya 21)
- **The ascetic at Ratnagrīva's court** — an emaciated brāhmaṇa ascetic appears in Kāñcī's assembly and answers the king's question of which god and tīrtha grant liberation with Puruṣottama and the Nīla mountain _(actors: Ratnagrīva, the brāhmaṇa ascetic)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ratnagriva-purushottama-kshetra.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Pātāla-khaṇḍa 17)
- **The asuras refuse Raji's price, the devas accept** — Raji demands the rank of Indra after victory; Prahlāda's asuras refuse ('Prahlāda is our lord') while the gods consent to the bargain _(actors: Raji, Prahlāda, devas, asuras)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/raji-and-his-sons.md) — (Vishnu Purana IV.9; Matsya Purana 24)
- **The birth of Paraśurāma told at Hāṭakeśvara** — the sixth avatāra's nativity is recounted among the kṣetra's glories, grafting the Bhārgava axe-wielder onto the Vaḍnagar ground _(actors: Paraśurāma, Jamadagni, Reṇukā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vadnagar-hatakeshvara-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Nāgara-khaṇḍa 166)
- **The boon that asks the hour of death** — instead of wealth or heaven Khaṭvāṅga asks the gods only how long he has to live, and is told a single muhūrta remains _(actors: Khaṭvāṅga, the devas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/khatvanga-one-muhurta.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9.9.43)
- **The Carmaṇvatī (Chambal) arises** — from the hides and blood of the thousands of animals dressed daily at Rantideva's kitchens, a stream is said to flow, named Carmaṇvatī, "she furnished with hides" — the modern Chambal _(actors: Rantideva, the river Carmaṇvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rantideva.md) — (Mahābhārata, Droṇa Parva ~67; cf. Bhāgavata 9.21)
- **The concealed mansion beneath the lake** — by his ascetic power Māṇḍakarṇi builds a hidden palace within the water where he dwells unseen with his five wives, their revels heard as music from the surface _(actors: Māṇḍakarṇi, five apsaras)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mandakarni-and-panchapsaras-lake.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Araṇya Kāṇḍa 11)
- **The consolation-grant of Daṇḍaka** — to appease the widowed Śūrpaṇakhā, Rāvaṇa grants her free range of the Daṇḍaka forest with Khara and 14,000 rākṣasas installed at Janasthāna as her guardians _(actors: Rāvaṇa, Śūrpaṇakhā, Khara, Dūṣaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/shurpanakha.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara Kāṇḍa)
- **The converted hunter is reborn as Vālmīki** — after a pious life of Rāma-japa the redeemed robber dies and is reborn as son of the anthill-covered sage Kṛṇu (Valmīka), destined to popularize Rāma's story in verse _(actors: the hunter, Śaṅkha, Kṛṇu/Valmīka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/valmiki-ratnakara-conversion.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Vaiśākhamāsa-māhātmya 21)
- **The curse ripens as Rāma and the loss of Sītā** — Bhṛgu's curse comes due when Viṣṇu is born as Rāma and endures years parted from his abducted consort, the frame running on to Durvāsa's prophecy of his reign _(actors: Viṣṇu-as-Rāma, Sītā, Durvāsa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigus-curse-on-vishnu.md) — (Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara 51)
- **The Daṇḍa curse empties the Daṇḍaka** — answering Rāma's question why the land is lifeless, Agastya tells how Ikṣvāku's son Daṇḍa violated Arajā and Śukra's seven-day dust-storm annihilated his kingdom into the deserted Daṇḍaka _(actors: Agastya, Rāma, Daṇḍa, Arajā, Śukra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shveta-corpse-king-and-agastya.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara Kāṇḍa 79–81)
- **The Daśaratha-kṛta Śani-stotra composed** — the king praises Saturn under a cascade of names (Kṛṣṇa, Piṅgala, Babhru, Raudra, Antaka, Saurī, Śanaiścara, Manda), and Śani vows that whoever recites the hymn shall be spared his affliction _(actors: Daśaratha, Śani)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dasharatha-shani-stotra-legend.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Nāgara-khaṇḍa, Daśaratha-Śanaiścara chapters)
- **The Devī's battlefield theophany outside Vārāṇasī** — ambushed by the usurpers, Sudarśana invokes the Goddess, who appears on her lion and slays Yudhājit and Śatrujit while the surviving kings hymn her _(actors: the Devī, Sudarśana, Yudhājit, Śatrujit)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sudarshana-shashikala-navaratri.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa, III.24–25)
- **The Dog at Rāma's Gate** — in the open court of Rāma-rājya a wounded dog comes to the palace gate demanding justice, and Rāma orders Lakṣmaṇa to admit the suppliant, refusing to turn away even a dog _(actors: Rāma, Lakṣmaṇa, the dog)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramas-justice-the-dog-and-the-mendicant.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara Kāṇḍa, prakṣipta sargas after 59; Baroda CE App. I) [late text]
- **The Dog Departs for Kāśī** — its case won, the fallen abbot-dog seeks no reinstatement but sets out for Vārāṇasī to fast unto death (prāya) in the sacred city _(actors: the dog)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramas-justice-the-dog-and-the-mendicant.md) — (Uttara Kāṇḍa, prakṣipta) [late text]
- **The estranged silence on Kailāsa before the Dakṣa-yajña** — Śiva passes into long samādhi and thereafter keeps aloof from Satī while conversing kindly, the stated backdrop to her uninvited visit to Dakṣa's sacrifice and self-immolation _(actors: Śiva, Satī, Dakṣa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sati-tests-rama.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Satī-khaṇḍa 25–26; Rāmcaritmānas, Bāl-kāṇḍa)
- **The family refuses to share the robber's sins** — sent home by the waylaid Seven Sages, the highwayman learns his wife and children will share his loot but not his guilt _(actors: the robber (Vālmīki-to-be), the Saptarṣi, his family)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/valmiki-ratnakara-conversion.md) — (Adhyātma Rāmāyaṇa, Ayodhyā-kāṇḍa 6)
- **The famine averted** — Daśaratha asks not for wealth but that Śani never again pierce the cart of Rohiṇī, sparing the earth the twelve-year drought, and Saturn grants it _(actors: Daśaratha, Śani)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dasharatha-shani-stotra-legend.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Nāgara-khaṇḍa)
- **The five apsaras sent to break the penance** — the gods (Agni foremost; popularly Indra) dispatch five foremost celestial nymphs to seduce Māṇḍakarṇi and disperse his merit _(actors: the devas, Agni, five apsaras)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mandakarni-and-panchapsaras-lake.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Araṇya Kāṇḍa 11)
- **The five hundred Rājeyas seize Indra's throne** — After Raji's death his sons demand Indra-ship as their inheritance and, refused, take heaven by force and appropriate Indra's sacrificial shares _(actors: sons of Raji, Indra, Nārada)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/raji-and-his-sons.md) — (Vishnu Purana IV.9; Bhagavata Purana 9.17; Matsya Purana 24)
- **The forty-eight-day famine fast** — a great famine strikes and Rantideva with his family takes neither food nor water for forty-eight days, until a little kheer, food, and water arrive at dawn to break the fast _(actors: Rantideva, his household)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rantideva.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9.21.5–6)
- **The four-quarter muster of Kiṣkindhā** — Sugrīva summons the world's vānaras, golāṅgūlas and ṛkṣas and dispatches search-parties for Sītā to the four directions _(actors: Sugrīva, Aṅgada, Hanumān, Jāmbavān)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/vanaras-and-rikshas.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Kiṣkindhā-kāṇḍa 37–45)
- **The fourfold charity** — about to eat, Rantideva gives his last meal in succession to a brāhmaṇa, a śūdra, and an outcaste with a pack of dogs, then surrenders his final water to a caṇḍāla begging for it _(actors: Rantideva, brāhmaṇa, śūdra, dog-keeper, caṇḍāla)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rantideva.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9.21.7–11)
- **The fourfold Mithilā wedding** — All four princes of Ayodhyā wed on a single day at Mithilā: Sītā–Rāma, Ūrmilā–Lakṣmaṇa, Māṇḍavī–Bharata, and Śrutakīrti–Śatrughna, the latter two being Kuśadhvaja's daughters _(actors: Bharata, Śatrughna, Māṇḍavī, Śrutakīrti, Rāma, Sītā, Lakṣmaṇa, Ūrmilā, Janaka, Kuśadhvaja, Vasiṣṭha, Viśvāmitra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/bharata-shatrughna-and-their-wives.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla-kāṇḍa 71–73)
- **The Gaṇḍakī māhātmya and the Śabara hunter's rescue** — at the Gaṇḍakī the king learns the śālagrāma's glory, and a dying Śabara robber is freed from Yama's servants by a śālagrāma laid on his chest and Rāma's name _(actors: Ratnagrīva, the learned brāhmaṇa, the Śabara hunter, Yama's messengers)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ratnagriva-purushottama-kshetra.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Pātāla-khaṇḍa 20)
- **The Guha boon fulfilled** — by Śiva's Śivarātri boon the hunter is reborn as Guha, Niṣāda chief of Śṛṅgaverapura, whom Rāma visits and befriends on the way to exile _(actors: Guha, Rāma, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shivaratri-hunter-legend.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṁhitā 40; Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Ayodhyā-kāṇḍa 50)
- **The invisible music of Pañcāpsaras** — journeying from Sutīkṣṇa's hermitage toward Agastya, Rāma hears song, instruments, and jingling ornaments rising from an empty-seeming lake and asks the marvel's cause _(actors: Rāma, Lakṣmaṇa, Sītā, the sage Dharmabhṛta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mandakarni-and-panchapsaras-lake.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Araṇya Kāṇḍa 11)
- **The Jāpaka and King Ikṣvāku** — a lifelong Gāyatrī-reciter wins emancipation as Death and the gods dispute his merit, and he and the Solar king Ikṣvāku each try to give the other the fruit of his deeds _(actors: the Jāpaka Brahmin, Sāvitrī, Yama, Ikṣvāku)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shanti-parva-minor-upakhyanas.md) — (MBh, Śānti Parva 12.189–193)
- **The king challenges Saturn** — Daśaratha strings his bow and threatens the graha to drive him from the cart of Rohiṇī, and Śani, delighted by a mortal's fearlessness, offers a boon instead of striking him down _(actors: Daśaratha, Śani)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dasharatha-shani-stotra-legend.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Nāgara-khaṇḍa, Daśaratha-Śanaiścara-saṃvāda)
- **The kirāta child transfigured on Mount Nīla** — a forest-tribe child eats a particle of rice offered to Viṣṇu in the hidden temple and gains a four-armed divine body, followed by his whole tribe _(actors: the kirāta child, the kirātas, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ratnagriva-purushottama-kshetra.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Pātāla-khaṇḍa 18)
- **The krauñca bird and the first śloka** — grief at a hunter's killing of a krauñca bird at the Tamasā bursts from Vālmīki as the first metrical verse _(actors: Vālmīki, the hunter, the krauñca pair)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/valmiki-ratnakara-conversion.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 2)
